ROP — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • Each business sells the system of record, the data network or the engineered device for one narrow profession: practice and financial management for large law firms, contract accounting for government contractors, agency management for property and casualty insurance brokers, load matching for freight brokers and truckers, water meters for municipal utilities.
  • The reoccurring line's growth rates are acquired revenue arriving, not organic volume. Procare closed February 2024 and Transact August 2024; with both fully in the prior-year base, Application Software reoccurring revenue grew 4.1% in Q1 2026 and 0.7% in Q2 2026, 2.5% for the half
  • Deltek serves 98% of the top 100 federal contractors and 95% of the top 100 architecture and engineering firms; Vertafore serves 100 of the top 100 brokers, 66 of the top 100 on an agency management system, and 96 of the top 100 property and casualty carriers; Strata plus Syntellis reach about 70% of US health systems
  • Price replaces churn; it does not expand take.
  • Price escalation holds. Seat counts are flat to shrinking in the two largest verticals. Payments stopped growing organically. Licences are in structural decline. Only CentralReach's clinician count compounds at a double-digit rate, and it is about 3% of the segment.
  • Realized incremental EBITDA margin over FY2023-25 was 40.2%, below the segment's own 42.5% average and below the "45%+ operating leverage" management published at the 2023 Investor Day
  • The disclosed split is narrower: transaction-priced revenue was 19.4% of the segment in FY2025 and 19.6% in Q2 2026, while subscription-type recurring revenue was 72.1% and 72.9%.
  • Taking Foundry at $85–105m, the DAT complex works out at roughly $290–420m of FY2025 revenue, **18–26% of the segment**
  • Sole-source position protected volume and renewal; it did not shorten the repricing lag.
  • Organic reoccurring revenue grew 5% for Roper and 5% for Network Software in FY2025. In Q2 2026 it declined 2% for Roper and 4% for Network Software, while reported reoccurring revenue still grew 8% and 20%. Every dollar of growth in the fastest-growing disclosed revenue category is now acquired.
competitive-advantage
  • The advantage is real today and defensible for now, with identified paths to erosion, and the excess return comes from the capital structure of the operating businesses, not from pricing power: management's own description is that price replaces churn.
  • Incremental after-tax return on incremental segment assets was 4.9% from FY2023 to FY2024 and 8.1% from FY2024 to FY2025. Against an approximate 8% after-tax cost of capital, the level is below and the increment straddles.
  • Over five years the margin gap has stopped widening. Application Software gross margin moved from 69.4% (FY2021) to 68.5% (FY2025) and segment operating margin from 26.8% to 26.8%, across an 89% revenue increase; on the amortization-free basis segment EBITDA margin declined from 43.4% (FY2023) to 42.4% (H1 2026).
  • The CEO said three businesses have a pricing meter that may have to be redesigned because agentic AI reduces the seat count it is billed against, and the two specified for near-term work are property and casualty insurance and legal — Vertafore and Aderant, the segment's two strongest franchises — with Deltek a possible third over a multi-year horizon
  • It breaks on price paid — Constellation ended FY2025 with $16,171m of assets on $11,623m of revenue, 1.39x, against Application Software's $25,373m on $4,483m, 5.66x, so Roper capitalizes roughly four times as much balance sheet per revenue dollar for a similar economic asset.
  • Network Software earns excess returns today and the position is defensible, but it is open to erosion and the strength is uneven across the nine businesses.
  • The operating economics are close to the best in software: 84.1% gross margin, 43.5% segment operating margin, capital expenditure of $9m on $1,601m of revenue, and negative tangible working capital. Between FY2020 and FY2025 the segment operating margin widened from 35.8% to 43.5%.
  • The neutral matching and data layer held its economics while the capital that tried to take the transaction itself was destroyed.
  • brokers post loads on DAT and on Truckstop and carriers subscribe to both, and every comparison guide found says so [web, trade press, accessed 2026-09-11]. That is consistent with a scale advantage where the larger board is worth more and the smaller board is still worth paying for, and inconsistent with exclusivity.
  • The direction reversed in the last three quarters. Segment operating margin went 45.2% (FY2024) to 43.5% (FY2025) to 41.0% (Q2 2026); adjusted EBITDA margin 56.1%, 54.1%, 50.9%.
  • The advantage is real and its arithmetic is exceptional, but its source is not the one management describes.
  • Pricing power is not the reason. TEP gross margin was 61.5% in 2020 and 56.9% in the first half of 2026, and it has not recovered to the 2020 level in any intervening year.
  • What holds the returns up is switching cost at two of the eight businesses plus an extraordinarily small capital base. At Northern Digital the tracking sensor is designed into another manufacturer's cleared medical device, so replacing it means the OEM re-validates its own product and, depending on the change, re-files.
  • The return premium is a capital-allocation outcome first and an operating outcome second. An acquirer paying a market price for these eight businesses today would not earn 30%: at 15x FY2025 segment operating profit a buyer would pay about $9.4bn and earn roughly 5% after tax on it.
  • The corporate centre adds value through three mechanisms that can be identified and priced, and at the entry multiples of the last five years most of what it adds is paid across to sellers. Roper earned a 30.8% free-cash-flow margin in FY2025 and 6.4% on the capital it deployed to assemble that cash flow.
  • The $290m excluded from every segment margin above is 3.7% of revenue and 11.5% of the three segments' combined operating profit of $2,525m. Less than half of it is the centre. Enterprise-wide stock-based compensation of $166m is equity pay for roughly 19,400 employees across 29 businesses, booked centrally because Roper does not allocate it, and the residual $124m is the centre's cash operating cost, 1.6% of revenue and 4.9% of combined segment operating profit.
  • Five to six points of spread on $9,356m of debt is $470m to $560m a year before tax, three to four times the centre's entire cash operating cost.
  • The surplus does not reach shareholders, because each mechanism improves what Roper can pay for an asset and it is spent in competitive processes where price clears. The entry yield on acquired forward EBITDA halved from 7.1% on Deltek in 2016 to 4.5% on CentralReach and Subsplash in 2025, and the multiple management quotes is the net multiple after the tax benefit, which puts the tax capacity explicitly into the bid.
  • The funnel measures a screen and not exclusivity. Roper's access is universal inclusion in the processes that fit its criteria, against a seller base the head of M&A put at about 20 large private equity firms holding 80% of the assets of interest, all of them running competitive auctions. The steepest cut below the initial criteria screen is a people screen: Roper drops 46 of the 71 businesses whose management it meets, and the last stage loses 10 of the 16 processes it has already committed diligence resource to.
  • Constellation Software tracks a database of about 40,000 opportunities against Roper's 1,200 annual targets, and reports that 40 to 70 large vertical-market-software businesses sell each year, that it is aware of about 80% of them, that it is invited into 16% of those processes, and that it has closed three large deals in 26 years. It lowered its own hurdle rate in 2019 for acquisitions requiring at least US$100m of equity in order to compete for them.
  • The outcomes are strong and the sample is management's own. The four businesses cost under $1.9bn in total, about 7% of the $27,009m deployed between FY2016 and FY2025, and none is among Roper's ten largest acquisitions. Three of the four were bought before Roper became a software acquirer at scale, and Foundry, the one showcased business whose record after the slide was printed is disclosed, declined by double digits through 2024.
  • Roper is the most acquisition-intensive of the nine named serial acquirers on whatever measure each publishes. It deployed 170% of ten-year free cash flow against a 66% to 112% range for the six SEC-filing industrials, 145% over the matched FY2021-FY2025 window against Constellation Software's 116%, Topicus's 86% and Halma's 68% to 85%, and 161% cumulatively since FY2007, the highest of thirteen SEC-filing serial acquirers. It also holds the most acquisition-derived balance sheet, 90.0% of total assets in goodwill and intangibles against a 53.6% to 80.8% range.
  • AMETEK isolates the cause. It held goodwill-inclusive return on invested capital at 13.1% to 13.2% across the same five years while acquiring continuously, on a decentralized portfolio of niche leaders run from a small centre, which is the structure Roper runs. It deploys 77% of free cash flow against Roper's 170% and pays 2x to 3x sales against Roper's 7x to 9x.
competitive-landscape
  • It holds in several lines and fails in others, and the failures are concentrated in Application Software.
  • The three largest businesses are roughly 47% of segment revenue and the top five roughly 66%. A segment described as twelve defensible niches is, in revenue terms, three large businesses and a tail.
  • The two vendors' own top-100 counts sum past 100 because large brokers that have rolled up agencies run multiple estates concurrently, and neither displaces the other at the top.
  • That limits the runway: Deltek's two highest-share markets leave little room for share gain, and the markets with headroom are the ones where Autodesk, Procore, Trimble and Sage are each larger than Deltek in total.
  • This is the line where "all of our competitors are smaller" does not hold.
  • Roughly $1.5–1.8bn of FY2025 segment revenue, on the order of a third of the segment, competes against a player larger than the Roper business facing it
  • Trade sources put DAT and Truckstop together above 85% of digital freight matching for carriers [web, accessed 2026-09-11], and the broker side is close to fully penetrated
  • What has changed is Dodge's balance sheet: S&P recorded ten consecutive quarters of revenue decline through Q2 2024 on "rising small to midsize business (SMB) customer churn" and "account management missteps," a selective default, an upgrade to CCC+ on 2024-12-06 after a distressed debt restructuring, leverage around 10x and $100m of new capital in November 2024
  • Neptune is smaller on revenue than both Badger Meter's utility water business and Xylem's smart metering line, though the Xylem line is not a like-for-like comparison because it spans electricity, gas and markets outside North America.
  • Ambu is roughly twice Verathon's size at group level and growing faster in the overlapping category. Both companies claim share gains in the same conversion, and since Roper discloses no Verathon growth rate the claims cannot be netted; if both hold, the single-use market is growing faster than either incumbent and the share shift is coming out of reusable scopes.
forward-model
  • Reported organic growth includes acquired businesses once they have been owned a full comparable period, so a deal closed in mid-2025 lifts *reported organic* growth in the second half of 2026 with no change in demand.
  • the base case reaches management's compounding claim without reaching its organic target: free cash flow per share compounds 14.7% a year from FY2025 to FY2033 and adjusted diluted EPS 13.7%, against "mid-teens," while same-store organic growth never exceeds 6.9% against a stated through-cycle 7.0-7.5% and an "entitled" rate north of 8%.
capital-allocation
  • The 45%+ algorithm has not been met in any year since it was published in March 2023. Incremental adjusted EBITDA margin was 42.3% in FY2023, 37.3% in FY2024 and 36.0% in FY2025, then 26.9% and 24.2% in the first two quarters of 2026, against a six-year FY2019-FY2025 measure of 41.0%.
  • Roper's own published leverage history has no precedent for it. The Investor Day chart plots every quarter from Q4 2016 to Q4 2022, and every peak there follows an acquisition and is repaid inside two years: 4.2x after Deltek, 4.7x after Vertafore, back to 2.7x by Q4 2022. The 3.4x reached in Q2 2026 followed $3.2bn of buybacks against roughly $50m of bolt-ons, with no acquired earnings to grow into.
ma-analyst
  • Roper paid $6.98 of acquisition cash for each $1 of in-year acquired revenue in FY2025, against Constellation Software's $1.37, and $4.00 against $0.99 on cumulative FY2023-FY2025 revenue added. Constellation bought 11 points of revenue growth for $1,513m while Roper bought 6.7 points for $3,290m.
  • Across five years the forward revenue multiple held at 9.1x, 9.1x and 9.4x while the forward EBITDA multiple rose from 18.4x to 19.3x to 22.0x, because the acquired EBITDA margin declined from 49.2% to 47.3% to 42.9% and the growth bought rose from mid-single-digit to 20%-plus. The rising EBITDA multiple is the margin of what Roper buys, and not a higher price per dollar of revenue.
management-claims
  • Delivered organic growth was 8% (FY2023), 6% (FY2024), 5% (FY2025) and is guided to about 6% for FY2026. The three completed years average 6.3% and the trend is down.
  • Reported reoccurring revenue nearly doubled over two years on acquired books; organic reoccurring revenue declined 2% in Q2 2026 across the software segments and grew 2.5% in H1 2026 in Application Software.
  • Against that, Neptune declined only modestly in the June 2026 quarter while Badger Meter's utility water sales declined 8.1% and Itron's revenue declined 7.2%, so the whole North American market contracted and Neptune contracted less.
  • The single-year path underneath decelerated: adjusted free cash flow grew 16% in FY2024 and 8% in FY2025. The FY2023-FY2025 compound rate on the computed series is 12.4%.
risks
  • Two points of gross-retention deterioration on the recurring base is about $90m of revenue, roughly 1.1 points of consolidated growth, before any net-retention offset.
  • If AI compresses those headcounts, the billable base contracts at 100% logo retention.
secular-trends
  • So the 73% of software revenue that recurs grew 7%, and the remaining 27% subtracted about 0.8 points. Every growth driver Roper describes outside subscriptions shrank in the latest quarter.
  • The end-market money grew 14.5% and the software supplier to that market grew in the low-to-mid single digits.
  • The recovery under way is a capacity contraction that lifts price. For DAT that is revenue-positive, because higher spot rates pull carriers back onto the spot market and onto load boards, but a price-led recovery caused by carriers leaving the industry is a different underwriting from a volume-led one, since the subscriber pool is the population of carriers.
  • Applying management's own range to FY2025 revenue, 200 to 400 basis points of added organic growth implies $158m to $316m of incremental annual revenue
  • At roughly a quarter to a third of its own defined addressable markets, and in markets management describes as already well vended, the share-gain contribution available to organic growth is limited by arithmetic.
  • The flagship share-gain case is also largely complete: Aderant's share "has gone from, like, 35% to 65%" since Roper acquired it (Q2 2026 call; the Q1 2026 version is 35-40% to 60-65%). Doubling share again is not available.
  • Roper grew faster than the two vertical-software roll-ups and slower than every vertical-software operator and every diversified industrial acquirer in the set.
  • The portfolio reshaping removed exposure to upstream oil and gas and to short-cycle industrial demand and replaced it with exposure to federal procurement policy, the trucking capacity cycle and municipal capital budgets.
  • A $900m base converting at 2 to 2.5x over a decade is roughly $90m to $135m of incremental annual revenue if spread evenly, about 1.1 to 1.7 points of growth on the FY2025 base, and it is not evenly spread: Deltek's Costpoint end-of-support date is Q1 2028.
  • A material part of Roper's reported organic growth acceleration in H2 2026 and into 2027 comes from fast-growing acquired businesses crossing the twelve-month ownership line, not from demand improving at the businesses already in the base. Reported organic growth at this company is partly a function of the M&A cadence one year earlier.
technology
  • Roper does not win on technology. It wins on owning the system of record in markets too small for a large competitor to bother with, and on the switching cost created when a customer configures its own rules, permissions and data inside that system.
  • Across three deals spanning the pre-AI and AI eras, the appraiser assigned the software itself 4-6% of the price and gave it less than half the useful life of the customer relationships
  • A competitor can write equivalent software; it cannot write the customer's twenty years of rules and historical data into it. Aderant added more than 220 law firms to Expert in the five years to March 2023, 39 of them in the AmLaw 200, and lost none.
  • The agentic layer separates incumbents from non-incumbents, and does not separate Roper from the competitor that matters most in its second-largest software franchise.
  • The barrier is design-in — once qualified, the sensor is in the OEM's regulatory submission, and changing it means re-validating the platform — plus a physics problem that does not yield to software, since sub-millimetre tracking inside a body in the presence of metal distortion is an accuracy problem.
value-chain
  • That structure puts the inference cost risk on Roper and books the revenue in the higher-margin line, the opposite of the payments arrangement where the processor sets price and Roper takes a share.
  • The binding constraint is the supply of vertical software businesses priced to clear Roper's return hurdle.
  • A 10x year-five EBITDA underwrite implies a pre-tax year-five return on deployed capital of about 10%.
  • Customers prepay $2,078m of deferred revenue ($1,907m current, $171m non-current) against $1,391m of minimum fixed-price purchase commitments, so net the chain hands Roper about $686m of interest-free funding.

Each business sells the system of record, the data network or the engineered device for one narrow profession: practice and financial management for large law firms, contract accounting for government contractors, agency management for property and casualty insurance brokers, load matching for freight brokers and truckers, water meters for municipal utilities.

earnings callsupports ✦ best
So for us, on software, we have always had a pricing mechanism in the ARR snowball, right? As a general matter, we attrit it. We have 95-or-so percent gross retention. So we're going to attrit 5-or-so percent. We're going to offset the vast majority of that at each business unit level with price. It's just in the algorithm. Peer: Convoy

websupports
In April 2022, Convoy raised $160 million in Series E funding, along with $100 million of venture debt. At the time, the business appeared to be headed toward a near-term IPO and saw participation from investors like T. Rowe Price, Fidelity, and Baillie Gifford. The freight market fell flat shortly after. By 2023, the IPO was off the table, and investors had sharply curtailed their appetite for risky, cash-burning investments. After total funding of more than $1 billion, including debt, Convoy was unable to secure additional financing.
— Sam Hillier, Transacted, Why Freight Brokerage Startup Convoy Ran Out of Road, 2023-10-25
websupports
We are in the middle of a massive freight recession and a contraction in the capital markets. This combination ultimately crushed our progress at the same time that it was crushing our logical strategic acquirer — it was the perfect storm.
— Dan Lewis, co-founder and CEO, Convoy (internal memo quoted by Transacted), Why Freight Brokerage Startup Convoy Ran Out of Road, 2023-10-25
websupports
Traditional trucking brokerages focus almost exclusively on net revenue, or topline earnings after accounting for payouts to drivers that transport the booked freight. Convoy opted to instead report a gross revenue figure, ignoring those payouts and posting results many times higher than what its industry peers would have reported for the same size business. FreightWaves’ Craig Fuller cited internal Convoy data reporting a 17% gross-to-net, or $170 million of “actual” revenue on Convoy’s initial 2022 target.
— Sam Hillier, Transacted, Why Freight Brokerage Startup Convoy Ran Out of Road, 2023-10-25
webcontradicts
As it winds down, it’s worth noting Convoy did deliver real change to the freight industry, even if it didn’t have the staying power. Automated brokerage opened up new capacity, improved safety, reduced cargo claims, lessened empty leg waste, and may have positioned ‘freight tech’ as a viable career path for talented engineers and developers.
— Sam Hillier, Transacted, Why Freight Brokerage Startup Convoy Ran Out of Road, 2023-10-25

webcontradicts
Convoy’s platform changes that. Built with hundreds of millions in venture funding, it is regarded as best-in-class by potential buyers. It handles the lot: finding capacity, matching loads, payments and execution. The system takes a cut for automating deals—like Amazon, not Craigslist.
— Craig Fuller, CEO, FreightWaves, Load-matching wars escalate as DAT snaps up Convoy, 2025-07-28

websupports

websupports

websupports

— Ahmad Qazi, founder, O Trucking LLC, DAT vs Truckstop Load Board: Full Comparison (2026), 2026-06-30
webqualifies

— Ahmad Qazi, founder, O Trucking LLC, DAT vs Truckstop Load Board: Full Comparison (2026), 2026-06-30
websupports

— Riley Quinn, Heavy Vehicle Inspection, Load Board Comparison 2026: DAT vs Truckstop vs Uber Freight, 2026-08-18
websupports

sellsidesupports
We view the transaction as distressed and tantamount to default because it provides the company’s lenders with less than what was originally promised under the credit agreement, including first-lien term loan lenders agreeing to exchange at a discount, second-lien term loan lenders converting to a more junior ranking, and switching to payment-in-kind interest payments from cash payments. Therefore, we lowered our issuer credit rating on the company to ‘SD’ (selective default) from ‘CCC+’ and our issue-level ratings on the company’s first- and second-lien term loans to ‘D’ from ‘CCC+’ and ‘CCC-‘, respectively.
— S&P Global Ratings, Dodge Construction Network LLC Downgraded To 'SD' From 'CCC+' Following Distressed Restructuring Transaction, 2024-11-21
sellsidesupports
The initial transaction raised $100 million of new capital in a new first-lien, first-out term loan from a group of existing first-lien term loan lenders, which was used to provide net liquidity of about $50 million and cover fees and expenses. This group of first-lien lenders, along with remaining first-lien lenders that participate in the second step of the transaction, will also roll $100 million of their existing holdings at a discount into that loan with the remaining exchanging into a new first-lien, second-out term loan.
— S&P Global Ratings, Dodge Construction Network LLC Downgraded To 'SD' From 'CCC+' Following Distressed Restructuring Transaction, 2024-11-21

Still, the company has made changes to its executive suite and has addressed many of the issues affecting EBITDA growth and cash flow generation over the past two years including integration challenges of the legacy Dodge business with The Blue Book, account management missteps that accelerated SMB churn, and customer invoice miscues that caused delayed collections across thousands of SMB customers.
— S&P Global Ratings, Dodge Construction Network LLC Upgraded To 'CCC+' From 'SD' Following New Debt Issuance; Outlook Stable, 2024-12-06
sellsidesupports
Despite improving liquidity, the company needs to further improve its cash flow generation to ensure the long-term sustainability of the business. Dodge’s total debt burden modestly grew because of the restructuring, and we expect very high leverage of about 10x in 2024. While we forecast healthy FOCF generation in 2025 and 2026 after negligible FOCF in recent years, the improvement is largely due to the PIK second-lien interest and our forecast for lower base interest rates in 2025 and 2026.
— S&P Global Ratings, Dodge Construction Network LLC Upgraded To 'CCC+' From 'SD' Following New Debt Issuance; Outlook Stable, 2024-12-06

The funnel measures a screen and not exclusivity. Roper's access is universal inclusion in the processes that fit its criteria, against a seller base the head of M&A put at about 20 large private equity firms holding 80% of the assets of interest, all of them running competitive auctions. The steepest cut below the initial criteria screen is a people screen: Roper drops 46 of the 71 businesses whose management it meets, and the last stage loses 10 of the 16 processes it has already committed diligence resource to.

webcontradicts
For many years, we have tracked large VMS acquisition prospects as a separate segment of the market. We have invested less than 10% of our FCFA2S in this segment, making only three large VMS acquisitions during our entire 26 year history. Between 40 and 70 large VMS businesses are sold each year. The vast majority of these transactions are marketed to prospective buyers by less than a dozen major merger and acquisition ("M&A") brokers. Over the last five years, we were aware of about 80% of the large VMS businesses that were sold, but their brokers only invited us to participate in 16% of the sales processes.
— Mark Leonard, President, Constellation Software, Constellation Software 2020 President's Letter, quoted in bizalmanac, 'A Better Combined Ratio', 2023-03-30

Constellation Software tracks a database of about 40,000 opportunities against Roper's 1,200 annual targets, and reports that 40 to 70 large vertical-market-software businesses sell each year, that it is aware of about 80% of them, that it is invited into 16% of those processes, and that it has closed three large deals in 26 years. It lowered its own hurdle rate in 2019 for acquisitions requiring at least US$100m of equity in order to compete for them.
